# The one list — every open decision, one register (started 2026-09-17; rows added through 2026-10-01; each row's status column is the owner of its status)

Folded from: the consolidation note (D-A…D-J), the three seat receipts (daemons D1–D4, author
C1 and its three decisions, run §4–§5), scout C (D1–D12), scout D (D-1…D-8), scout E (E-1…E-10
and its eight slices), scout F (F-1…F-12 and the seven squares), the LCNF survey (§7, the LLVM
steer). Where two or more said the same thing under different names, one row; the sources are
listed so the reasoning can be found. **Owner** = a ruling is needed; **do** = no decision
content, scheduled. Recommendations are the coordinator's, after reading all six inputs.

**Reviewed 2026-09-18 against HEAD** (after the census, `fold_of`, the checker as one
`Except`-valued fold, the `explain` and `effTy` callers slices, and the first Schema cuts): a
status column, verified row by row against the tree with file and line; rows 12, 13, 30 (for
`denote` and `effTy`), 36, 37, 38 closed; rows 39 (the Schema wipe as one ruling) and 40 (the
callers phase, continued) added; the order re-cut at the foot. The owner's steer of 2026-09-17
("coherence of the algebra is the metric, not consumers; convert to folds where the shape
allows, delete at a good place") is applied to the rows argued from consumer counts (4, 9, 12).

Corrected on implementation:** the recommendation's premise was wrong. The checker types a service by its key's static type (`nativeServiceTy`; `provideService` checks the value is a subtype of it), not by the value in scope, so the static alternative is what the checker uses and it was implemented instead: no world table, no per-fiber environment. Landed: a `Context` handle's fit carries `ServicesOk` (every present service fits its key's static type); `setContext` requires it; the store protocol takes the program source and a memo lookup certifies its layer's checked error type, a hit's deferred declared at the context handle and that error. Controls: `service_admitted` (loaded code of a service read), `memoAwait_typed` (`Test/Program/LoadedAdmission.lean`). A ref-typed service is refused, not mistyped (its nested handle is not re-checked). The state predicate owes the fibers' contexts and the memo entries' deferreds (slice 5 §3.5/§3.6) |

| 190 | The loop and generator hook contract (`E4-TYPED-CE-036`, `-037`, `-038`; G2) | Three gaps, kernel-checked at `ed83ea23` (`docs/research/2026-10-02-claude-lead/witnesses/LoopProtocols.lean`). (a) The mutually inductive protocols refuse safe nontermination: the checker-admitted `iterate` with an always-true test has no `LoopProtocol` at any input type admitting `unit` (`Endless.no_protocol`), and the fiber its entry leaves has no `CodeOk` at any world (`no_code_after_entry`), so `step_loop` is unprovable over the current contract for programs with such a loop. (b) The loop operation's precondition ignores its cursor: `fiberPre`'s loop arm reads only `PointTyped`; a checked Boolean-cursor loop entered with `unit` installs `badShapeExit` (`Cursor.entry_current`). (c) The protocols state the reference interpreter's step while the machine runs the view-aware one (`interpRAt root m.completedExits`): a loop frame whose point's own view answers an await inline has a protocol, yet the machine's hook laws fail on it at the empty view (`View.no_hookLaws`, `View.walk_untyped`). Proposed: (a) the protocols in their postfixed (greatest-fixed-point) form: a frame's protocol is an invariant containing it and closed under one hook step (every resumed body or code typed at one intermediate type, its tail again in the invariant, at every later world; the error-column equality and requirement-row condition kept), the standard coinduction principle; the old inductive protocol embeds one way, the endless loop is admitted, an ill-typed body is still refused. (c) The step obligation quantifies over the completed views typed at the step's world (the construction post's clause), so `HookLawsAt` for `interpRAt root C` follows from that clause on `C`; `M5Hooks.hookLaws_interpR` (the bare interpreter, which the machine does not run) is replaced by a goal for the machine's interpreter (owner, at the ruling: "This is all greenfield"; no history copy is kept). A theorem's status is read from its dependencies: proved when it reaches no goal, proved modulo the goals the walk stops at otherwise, and open when it is a goal. Guardrails: (1) `sorry` appears only as the whole body of a declared goal; the token stays banned in source, and Lean's sorry warning is off only inside the command. (2) A goal is a theorem of a proposition, in `Effect4.Laws` or a `Test` fixture; the `Effect4` root reaches none, so no compiled code holds a `sorry`. (3) The axiom gate's ceiling stays `[propext, Quot.sound]`; a declaration reaches `sorryAx` only through goals, and the gate reports the declarations that rest on goals. (4) A registry claim or a requirement's top node counts as proved only when it reaches no goal. (5) A goal a counterexample refutes is deleted or restated. ## Derived forms, queues and the shared foundations (2026-10-05)

Ten groups of rulings, all of 2026-10-05.

- **Rows 214 to 218, and row 130's ruling.** The owner ratified the seven recommendations of the
  derived forms plan in session ("all's recommended").
- **Rows 219 to 234.** The owner ratified Codex's recommendations "as recommended" and had Codex
  relay them. The relay is `coordinator-note.txt` in
  `docs/research/2026-10-05-codex-foundation-packet/`. The same day the owner said that Codex's
  relays count as coming from the owner, and then gave the word in session to land them.
- **Rows 235 to 237.** The owner answered the coordinator's five follow-up questions in session
  ("yes to all five, go with your recommendations").
- **Rows 238 and 239, and row 227's amendment.** The owner signed off the waiting design's three
  points in session ("yes to all three"), after Codex's review of that note.
- **Rows 240 to 243.** The owner ratified the Queue contract's three choices and its refused
  operations in session ("go ahead with all three", "ratify as recommended").
- **Rows 244 to 248.** The owner ratified the mask's three directions in session ("yes to all
  three"), after Codex's review of the mask's second note. The same message corrected the
  coordinator's reading of "the TypeScript package" (row 247). It also ruled the migration to
  4.0.1 in increments (row 248).
- **Rows 249 to 252.** The owner answered the coordinator's four open points in session: keep
  `void` at `Scope.close`, download the driver, land Codex's follow-ups, and the order after
  the fold.
- **Row 253.** The owner spoke on the migration in session, and told Codex the same day that
  its mechanics are fine. Codex relayed that phrase.
- **Row 254.** The owner spoke on dogfooding in session, after Codex's review of the acceptance
  programs.
- **Row 255.** The owner ratified the five proposals of the Queue's steps design in session
  ("yes, go with your recommendations on the five proposals"), after Codex's two reviews of
  that note. The same hour the owner allowed its seat as a third one (row 237's amendment).

The evidence behind rows 219 to 234 is finite runs, finite models, source reading and primary
literature. None of it is a Lean proof. Each row's obligations are open parts of the requirement
rows in the semantics registry (`tools/Tools/SemanticsRegistry.lean`), and
`docs/research/2026-10-05-claude-lead/foundation-contracts.md` consolidates them in one table.

| 221 | The shared waiting wrapper | One attempt, registration and retry infrastructure is shared. Each module owns its enrolment and its cancellation. A body is admitted by a checked body profile that tracks every dynamic cell access; "any body" is withdrawn. The decision to wait and the registration are one admitted transition. A wake invites another attempt and reserves no result. The invariant says where an outstanding notification lives at every transition: store debt, queued commands, dispatcher work, or the receiver's accepted continuation. The logical request, the await token and the wake batch's phase are three different things. Acceptance traces, each with its positive control: a notification before the await; a cancellation after the selection and before the delivery; a late delivery with an old token after rearming | `docs/research/2026-10-05-claude-lead/transactions-and-clock.md` F5 and proposal 1; `docs/research/2026-10-05-claude-lead/tx-probes/TxModel.lean`; `docs/research/2026-10-05-codex-foundation-packet/foundation-audit.md` §2 | owner | **ruled 2026-10-05 (as recommended)**; its design is owed before the first Queue slice (row 233) |
| 222 | Cancellation of an operation that waits | When cancellation wins before consumption, the request is withdrawn and nothing is consumed. After consumption the state stays committed, even when the caller is interrupted before its continuation receives the value. The observation records four things apart: the commitment, the operation's exit, the entry of the caller's continuation, and the fiber's exit. No law says that an interrupted take consumed nothing, or that every committed result reaches the caller's code; masking gives no such guarantee. An acquisition whose lifetime must be protected, such as a permit, keeps that lifetime inside a bracketed operation with installed cleanup. Evidence: a protected `Queue.take` consumes and reaches its result, and the restored mask interrupts before the caller continues, on rc.112, 4.0.1 and Effect 3.22.2 (five controls on each) | `docs/research/2026-10-05-codex-foundation-packet/atomic/cancel-return/README.md`; `docs/research/2026-10-05-claude-lead/queue-probes/cancel-return.ts` and its three outputs; `docs/research/2026-10-05-codex-foundation-packet/literature/tasks/contracts.md` | owner | **ruled 2026-10-05 (as recommended)**; not landed |
| 223 | The atomic body (the first profile of row 80) | An atomic body is a restricted fragment of `Eff`: pure terms, transactional reads and writes of existing cells, success, failure, retry, and flat composition. The first profile excludes allocation, host effects, recovery from a failure inside the body, general loops, and calls that are unresolved or reentrant. Nesting is flat: only the outer attempt commits or discards. The commit's bookkeeping, the notifications included, finishes before any receiver's continuation runs; a body with an inline delivery is not one region. Reads and writes are recorded in their dynamic order, and no static overestimate stands in where the wake behaviour is claimed. Transition bodies are shared across modules. Fair composition of tickets that are enrolled apart is outside the first profile (the opposing-ticket cycle). Evidence: under `uninterruptible` and `PreventSchedulerYield`, a waiter of a completed `Deferred` still runs inline and sees the middle state, on rc.112 and 4.0.1 | `docs/research/2026-10-05-codex-foundation-packet/review.md` §B and §D; `docs/research/2026-10-05-codex-foundation-packet/foundation-audit.md` §3; `docs/research/2026-09-19-stm-scout.md`; `docs/research/2026-10-05-claude-lead/tx-probes/TxModel.lean` (`opposingTickets`); rows 80 and 84 | owner | **ruled 2026-10-05 (as recommended)**; its design is owed before the first consumer; row 80's version erasure and target agreement stay open |
| 224 | Alternatives inside a transaction | A fallback on retry only, the rule of Harris et al.: the left branch's writes are discarded, the enclosing writes are kept, and when both branches retry the wait covers both branches' dependencies. | 240 | Every signal of the Queue is posted (under rows 220 and 238) | Each signal of the Queue goes through row 238's posted helper: a taker's, a peeker's, an awaiter's and a waiting offerer's. An offer is still accepted by the step that frees room, and that step decides the offerer's answer. Only the delivery of that answer is posted: the helper carries the decided answer, and nothing reads it from a later state of the queue. A withdrawal after the accepting step removes nothing, so the message stays accepted, and the offerer may exit interrupted before it reads its answer. The native queue resumes a waiting offerer inside the take that frees room, and the offerer's code runs there. The difference is signed in the Queue's profile (D1): one offerer waits and then offers again, and one fiber runs take, take, poll with no yield. The contract answers `[1, 2, none]`, and both builds answer `[1, 2, some(3)]`. Reason: the signalling fiber runs none of the receiver's code. | 274 | The fiber numbers of the truth lane's compared fields | The Lean face writes a fiber under its number in the recorder's order of first sight. It does so in every compared field that holds a fiber: each row of the schedule, and the exits of the fork run and of the sync run. The contract already compared the schedule over fiber indices in first-seen order, and it called a fiber id a position with no meaning across the faces (`Test/contracts/faces.contract.md`, §4, quantifiers 2 and 6). ## The order (milestone before the MCP server; state/refinement review before the concrete ledger pin)

**History, written 2026-09-19 and kept as the dated record.** Each row's status column above is the
owner of its status; a row named open here may have been ruled or landed since (rows 20, 21 and 86
are ruled; row 39 landed on 2026-10-01; rows 134–149 came from the formal pass).

The owner paused implementation on 2026-09-19 to reconcile the completed research. The proposed
D0–D7 sequence in the refinement plan refines the milestone work below; rows 78–83 are still
open. Generic language prerequisites already authorized by rows 42–43 precede the proof arms
that depend on them. Fast-container and all-backend implementations are not new prerequisites
for the typed-state milestone.

1. **Now, no rulings needed** — the small rows verified open above: 24, 16 (`Await`), 17, 18
   with 23, 5 restated at `Ty`. (Row 40 closed and row 34 ruled out on 2026-09-18: the fold work
   stops here; the thirteen exemptions are tracked, not converted.)
2. **Row 39, one ruling** — the Schema wipe. It closes 4 and 9, takes `Image` out of row 10's
   count, and ends with `render` leaving `Shape.lean`. (Landed 2026-10-01: the move was deletion
   only, so row 8's `ShapeDoc.document` fix did not land in it and stays open in
   `Schema/OfShape.lean`.) Then row 10 (one `Val → Json`) over what remains.
3. **Row 41, the milestone** — the typed-state invariant on the reference machine, in the corrected order
   of `docs/research/history/post-phase-c-synthesis.md` §§5–6: relational interfaces and valid-world
   transport, protocols before predicate assembly, stack/delivery, denotation and initialization,
   transition preservation, then transfer through `BMeans` to the compiled machine's exits.
   Direct proofs and an explicit inventory suffice while the semantic structure is changing;
   new gates are added only when they remove demonstrated repeated work.
4. **Group D** (rows 26, 27, 28, 31, 32, 29; row 30's `compileEff` ruling when row 27's root order
   reaches it): the TypeScript rules, the recipe, rule coverage, the rung-3 reader, the vendoring
   order, then the profile/legalization/IR refactor with the TypeScript printer as its first
   customer. The visual-plane research (the owner's layout and design-system documents, the
   2026-09-17 plan and its two reviews) is scouted against the printer's substrate before this
   group starts, so the TypeScript printer and the visual plane target one document algebra or
   knowingly two.
5. **Group B** (rows 14, 15, with 18 and 23): the digest, the Lean MCP driver over the `ToolSpec`
   table; then 2, 7, 11 — names at the boundary, handles, the reviver table; then the ledger and
   the pool dogfoods. Moved after group D on the owner's steer of 2026-09-18: the server would
   freeze a wire shape that LCNF handling and the milestone still change.
6. **Group C and the rest** (rows 1 with 3, 19, 20, 21, 22): the AST as second carrier when row 32
   step 2 reads a foreign schema, `Ty.app` with it, the file moves and daemon words, the machine
   edit, the two confirmations.

Owner rows open after the review (as of 2026-09-19): 1, 2, 7, 10, 11, 14, 15, 19, 20, 21, 22, 26, 27, 28, 29, 30
(`compileEff` only), 32, and the proposed policy rows 78–85; 4 and 9 are folded into 39, which is ruled.
Row 41 is ruled but unfinished. Coordinator design questions 86–88 are open technical

future upstream publication still separate (row 53).
